\subsection{Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:s6-1112}: Part~I with a finite-order twist}\label{subsec:s6-twist}

\paragraph{The data.} Fix $\psi\in\mathcal F_{\rm CM}$. By the definition of $\mathcal F_{\rm CM}$, $\psi$ is
the primitive character inducing $\overline\alpha\eta$, where $\eta$ is a character of the ray class group
modulo some integral ideal $\mathfrak m$. Thus
\begin{equation}\label{eq:d191}
 \psi((n))=\overline{\alpha(n)}\,\eta((n))
\end{equation}
for every primary $n$ prime to $3\mathfrak m$. Let $S$ be the set of prime ideals that divide $6\mathfrak m$.
If $n\equiv n'\pmod{\mathfrak m}$ are primary and prime to $S$, then $n/n'\equiv1\ \mathrm{mod}^\times\,\mathfrak m$
(multiplicatively), so $\eta((n))=\eta((n'))$. Put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:d192}
 \nu(n)=\overline{\eta((n))}\quad(n\text{ primary, prime to }S),\qquad\nu(n)=0\quad\text{otherwise}.
\end{equation}
For a primary prime $\pi\notin S$ this gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:s6-euler}
 \alpha(\pi)\nu(\pi)=\overline{\overline{\alpha(\pi)}\eta((\pi))}=\overline{\psi((\pi))}.
\end{equation}
Fix $L_S\in\OO$ whose prime factors are exactly the primes of $S$, and which is divisible by $72$, by a
generator of $\mathfrak m$, and by the moduli of the finitely many ray characters
$n\mapsto\chi_n\bigl(\upsilon\prod_{\mathfrak p\in S}\pi_{\mathfrak p}^{v_{\mathfrak p}}\bigr)$
of~\cite[Lemma~4.1]{OAI78}. Here $\upsilon$ is a unit, $0\le v_{\mathfrak p}\le5$, and
$\pi_{\mathfrak p}$ is a fixed generator of $\mathfrak p$. Let $\mathfrak G_S$ be the group of classes in
$(\OO/L_S\OO)^\times$ congruent to $1$ modulo $3$. These are the classes of the primary elements prime to $S$.
When $S$ consists of the primes above $6$, one may take $L_S=72$, and $\mathfrak G_S$ is the group
$\mathfrak G$ of Section~\ref{sec:poisson}. In general $\mathfrak G_S$ has four properties.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(G1)] $G$, $\mathcal R$ and $x\mapsto\chi_x(-1)$ are functions of classes modulo $4$
 (Section~\ref{subsec:b2-cm}). Reduction $\mathfrak G_S\to(\OO/4\OO)^\times$ is a homomorphism, so they are
 functions on $\mathfrak G_S$, and~\eqref{eq:b2-quotient} holds on $\mathfrak G_S$.
\item[(G2)] $\nu$ is a character of $\mathfrak G_S$, by the congruence property just proved.
\item[(G3)] The fixed-numerator characters above are characters of $\mathfrak G_S$.
\item[(G4)] $(\lambda/x)_3$ depends only on $x\bmod9$ for primary $x$, and $9\mid L_S$.
\end{enumerate}
The twisted rows are
\begin{equation}\label{eq:d193}
 A_u^\nu(D)=\sum_n\muk(n)\alpha(n)\nu(n)\chi_n(u)W\Bigl(\frac{\N(n)}D\Bigr),
\end{equation}
over primary $n$, with $u\in\OO$. The factor $\nu(n)$ restricts $n$ to elements prime to $S$. For $\nu=\ind$
and $S$ the primes above $6$ these are the rows~\eqref{eq:Au} of Part~I.

\begin{proposition}[Twisted mean square]\label{prop:s6-ms}
For every fixed $0<\vartheta\le1/10$ and $\varepsilon>0$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:d194}
 \sum_{0<\N(u)\le D^{1+\vartheta}}|A_u^\nu(D)|^2\ll_{\psi,W,\vartheta,\varepsilon}D^{2+\vartheta+\varepsilon}.
\end{equation}
As in~\eqref{eq:MS}, the constant is controlled by a finite smoothness norm of $W$.
\end{proposition}

This is the CM form of~\cite[Proposition~3.1]{OAI1112}, and~\eqref{eq:MS} is the case $\nu=\ind$. The
proof needs the CM transformation formula at a general modulus.

\begin{lemma}[The transformation formula at a general modulus]\label{lem:s6-reflection}
Let $L\in\OO$ have prime factors exactly the primes of $S$, with $72\mid L$, and let $\mathfrak G_L$ be the
group of classes in $(\OO/L\OO)^\times$ congruent to $1$ modulo $3$. Let $\xi_0$ be a character of
$\mathfrak G_L$, extended by zero to elements that are not primary or not prime to $S$. Let $\mathcal P$ be a
finite set of primary primes outside $S$, with $0\le j_\pi\le5$, put
$\Psi=\xi_0\prod_{\pi\in\mathcal P}\chi_\pi^{j_\pi}$, and define $T(X;\Psi)$ by~\eqref{eq:T}. Then
Proposition~\ref{prop:b3-reflection} holds with three changes:
\begin{itemize}
\item the number of terms is at most $\N(L)\,2^{|\mathcal P|}$;
\item $c_0$ is an associate of the reduced denominator of $\lambda^2h_0/L$, so its prime factors lie in $S$;
\item $\psi$ is an additive character modulo $\lambda^3c_0$.
\end{itemize}
Lemma~\ref{lem:b3-uniform} holds with $h_0\in\OO/L\OO$ and $M=\lambda^{12}L^4$. Lemma~\ref{lem:b3-decay} does
not involve $L$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Follow Section~\ref{sec:b3-proof}. In Step~1 put $\phi(x)=(\lambda/x)_3\,\xi_0(x)$ if $x$ is primary and prime
to $S$, and $\phi(x)=0$ otherwise. Then $\phi$ is $L$-periodic, by~(G4) and $9\mid L$. By~\eqref{eq:theta-coeff}, the selection
statement after it, and the vanishing of $\phi$ at elements not prime to $S$, the coefficient of
$\Theta_\Psi$ at $\ell$ vanishes unless $\lambda^3\ell=nb^3$ with $n$ squarefree and $n,b$ primary and prime
to $S$. Its value is still~\eqref{eq:b3-coeff}. The finite Fourier expansion~\eqref{eq:b3-translates} holds
with $h_0\in\OO/L\OO$, and $\sum_{\mathbf h}c_{\rm F}(\mathbf h)=\phi(0)\prod_\pi\chi_\pi(0)^{j_\pi}=0$.

Step~2 quotes facts (a)--(e) from~\cite[App.~A.2]{OAI1112}. They are proved there for any nonzero $L$ with
prime divisors in $S$ that is divisible by every prime of $S$, by the conductor of the fixed character, and
by sufficiently high powers of the primes above $2$ and $3$. Since $72\mid L_S$, the powers of $2$ and $\lambda$
in $L_S$ are at least those of $72$, which Appendix~\ref{app:reflection} uses; the argument of~\cite[App.~A.2]{OAI1112}
uses them through congruences modulo $q^{v_q(Mc_0)}$, with $M=\lambda^{12}L^4$. This covers the reduced denominators $c=c_0r$ with $c_0$ as stated, the matrices $g$, $H$,
$g_1$ and the label $\sigma$, the multiplier $\kappa(g_1)=\kappa_0\prod_{\pi\mid r}\chi_\pi(a)^2$
(\cite[(A.10)]{OAI1112}), the phase~\eqref{eq:b3-crt} with $\psi$ modulo $\lambda^3c_0$
(\cite[(A.11)]{OAI1112}), and the local identity~\eqref{eq:b3-local} at the primes of $\mathcal P$, which lie
outside $S$ (\cite[(A.12)]{OAI1112}).

Steps~3--6 use the data of Step~2 only through~\eqref{eq:b3-automorphy}, \eqref{eq:b3-crt},
\eqref{eq:b3-local} and $|c_{\rm F}(\mathbf h)|\le|\widehat\phi(h_0)|\le1$. The derivative, the Mellin
transforms, the functional equation and the assembly do not involve $L$. The value $L=72$ was used only in
the count $\N(72)=5184$ of the groups $(h_0,\mathcal A)$ and in the description $c_0\mid8\lambda^2$.
Lemma~\ref{lem:b3-uniform} is~\cite[Lemma~6.3]{OAI1112}, which is proved for this general $L$ at the end
of~\cite[App.~A.2]{OAI1112}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:s6-ms}]
Run the proof of~\eqref{eq:MS} in Sections~\ref{sec:poisson}--\ref{sec:descent} and
Appendix~\ref{app:reflection}, with four substitutions.
\begin{itemize}
\item[(S1)] Every column-type variable runs over primary elements prime to $S$ instead of prime to $6$. These
 are the columns $n,z_i,m_i,x_i$, the cube indices $b$ of~\eqref{eq:T} and $h$ of~\eqref{eq:inversion}, and
 the divisors $g,e_0,v_0,r_0,f,C,t,e,w,r,j,f'$ of Sections~\ref{sec:poisson} and~\ref{sec:descent}. The
 row and frequency variables ($u$, $k$, $y$, the Poisson frequencies, $h_2$, $k'$) still run over $\OO$.
\item[(S2)] $\mathfrak G$ becomes $\mathfrak G_S$; the characters $\xi,\xi_1,\xi'$ range over the
 characters of $\mathfrak G_S$, and $|\mathfrak G|=432$ becomes $|\mathfrak G_S|$.
\item[(S3)] Appendix~\ref{app:reflection} is used at $L=L_S$, in the form of Lemma~\ref{lem:s6-reflection}.
\item[(S4)] In Step~2 of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:b2-reduction},~\eqref{eq:paired} is replaced
 by~\eqref{eq:b2-forward} with $\xi_1=\overline\nu$.
\end{itemize}
We check the written-out CM arguments in which $6$, $72$ or $\mathfrak G$ occur. Every step that Part~I cites
from~\cite{OAI1112} is proved there for a general finite set $S$ and a general fixed finite-order twist.

\emph{Section~\ref{subsec:b2-cm}, inputs.} Formula~\eqref{eq:poisson-exact} is~\cite[Lemma~21]{QF} for a
primitive character modulo $z_1z_2$ with exclusion ideal $g$; enlarging the excluded set changes nothing in
it. The identities~\eqref{eq:gauss} and~\eqref{eq:b2-g1g2} hold prime by prime. Identity~\eqref{eq:b2-quotient}
holds on $\mathfrak G_S$ by~(G1). The exclusion bound~\eqref{eq:b2-remove} is~\cite[Lemma~4.4]{OAI1112}, which is
stated there for a general $S$.

\emph{Lemma~\ref{lem:b2-paired}} holds for characters $\xi,\xi_1$ of $\mathfrak G_S$, with $|c_\xi(\xi_1)|\le1$
and $|\ell_{\xi_1}(\xi)|\le1$. Its proof uses only four facts:~\eqref{eq:b2-crt} (the Chinese remainder theorem
and sextic reciprocity for coprime $z_1,z_2$ prime to $6$);~\eqref{eq:b2-two} (prime by
prime);~\eqref{eq:b2-quotient} on the group; and that $\xi,\xi_1$ are characters of a group through which $G$
factors.

\emph{Proposition~\ref{prop:b2-reduction}.} In Step~1 the coefficient of the pair $(n_1,n_2)$ acquires
$\overline{\nu(n_1)}\nu(n_2)=|\nu(g)|^2\,\overline{\nu(z_1)}\nu(z_2)=\overline{\nu(z_1)}\nu(z_2)$, since $g$ is
prime to $S$. The zero frequency still has coefficient $|W(\N(g)/D)|^2$, so $Z$ is unchanged, with $g$ prime
to $S$. In Step~2,~\eqref{eq:b2-forward} with $\xi_1=\overline\nu$ gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:d195}
 \muk(z_1)\muk(z_2)\overline{\alpha(z_1)}\alpha(z_2)\overline{\nu(z_1)}\nu(z_2)\,
 \gamma(\overline{\chi_{z_1}}\chi_{z_2})=\sum_\xi c_\xi(\overline\nu)\,a_\xi(z_1)\overline{a_\xi(z_2)},
\end{equation}
and therefore $\mathcal M_D-Z=\sum_\xi c_\xi(\overline\nu)\mathcal S_\xi$, with $\mathcal S_\xi$
as in~\eqref{eq:b2-Sxi}. For $z_1=z_2=1$ the identity reads $1=\sum_\xi c_\xi(\overline\nu)$. Steps~3--5 use
the coefficients only through~\eqref{eq:b2-twisted} and $|a_\xi|\le1$, which hold for every character of
$\mathfrak G_S$. The bijection of Step~3 and the scales of Step~4 do not see $S$. The number of characters,
$|\mathfrak G_S|$, enters only the constant.

\emph{Sections~\ref{sec:theta} and~\ref{sec:sieve}.} For a character $\xi$ of $\mathfrak G_S$, the twist
$\Psi(n)=\xi(n)\chi_n(k)\chi_n(f)^4$ has the form $\xi_0\prod_{\pi\in\mathcal P}\chi_\pi^{j_\pi}$, where
$\mathcal P$ is the set of primes outside $S$ dividing $kf$ and $\xi_0$ is a character of $\mathfrak G_S$:
$\xi_0$ collects $\xi$, the unit and reciprocity factors, and the factors of $k$ at the primes of
$S$~\cite[(6.17)]{OAI1112}, which are characters of $\mathfrak G_S$ by~(G1) and~(G3). The proof of the completed
estimate~\eqref{eq:completed} in Section~\ref{subsec:b4-completed} then applies with
Lemma~\ref{lem:s6-reflection} in place of Proposition~\ref{prop:b3-reflection} and
Lemma~\ref{lem:b3-uniform}. Lemma~\ref{lem:b3-decay} is unchanged. The large sieve~\eqref{eq:qls} is applied
with rows prime to $S$; the dual columns, the theta indices, need not be prime to $S$, and~\eqref{eq:qls} allows
arbitrary squarefree columns. The bound for
squarefree rows is~\cite[Lemma~6.6]{OAI1112}, whose proof fixes the part of the row at $S$ and at the primes of
$g$ for a general $S$.

\emph{Section~\ref{sec:descent}.} Identity~\eqref{eq:inversion} holds with $h$ prime to $S$. Cube removal,
Lemma~\ref{lem:b4-cube}, is~\cite[Lemma~5.3]{OAI1112}, proved for a general $S$, with~\eqref{eq:completed}. In
the transfer estimate, Lemmas~\ref{lem:b4-first}, \ref{lem:b4-second-id} and~\ref{lem:b4-second} use the Poisson
formula of~\cite[Lemma~21]{QF}, the paired identities of Lemma~\ref{lem:b2-paired} (now over $\mathfrak G_S$),
the exclusion bound~\eqref{eq:b2-remove} and~\cite[Lemma~B.2]{OAI1112}. The finitely many $\xi_1$ are the
characters of $\mathfrak G_S$. The induction of Section~\ref{sec:descent} uses only cube removal, the transfer
estimate, counting and $|a_\xi|\le1$.

Hence~\eqref{eq:finalenergy} holds for every character $\xi$ of $\mathfrak G_S$, which is the
hypothesis~\eqref{eq:b2-hyp} over $\mathfrak G_S$, and the modified Proposition~\ref{prop:b2-reduction} gives
the assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:s6-1112}]
\emph{Extraction.} Let $H=D^{1+\vartheta}$ and $Y=H^{1/6}$. Let $\mathcal P_Y$ be the set of primary primes
$\pi\notin S$ with $Y/2<\N(\pi)\le Y$, and $J=|\mathcal P_Y|$. Lemma~\ref{lem:b1-extract} holds with $u_0=1$,
$N_0=1$ and this $\mathcal P_Y$. In it, $A_u^\nu$ replaces $A_u$, $A_1^\nu(D)$ replaces $S_d(D)$, and the mean
square of Proposition~\ref{prop:s6-ms} replaces $M(D,H)$. Its proof uses only
$|\muk(n)\alpha(n)\nu(n)|\le1$, $\chi_n(\pi^6)=\ind_{\pi\nmid n}$, the distinctness of the rows $\pi^6$, and
lattice-point counting. Since $S$ is finite, the prime number theorem for primes $\equiv1\pmod3$~\cite{Lan03} gives
$J\ge Y/(5\log Y)$ for $Y\ge Y_0(S)$. As in the proof of~\eqref{eq:Sbound}, Proposition~\ref{prop:s6-ms} then
gives, for every $\varepsilon>0$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:s6-bound}
 A_1^\nu(D)=\sum_n\muk(n)\alpha(n)\nu(n)W\Bigl(\frac{\N(n)}D\Bigr)\ll_{\psi,W,\varepsilon}D^{11/12+\varepsilon}
 \qquad(D\ge1).
\end{equation}

\emph{Zeros.} Let $W$ be supported in $[a,b]\subset(0,\infty)$. Then $A_1^\nu(D)=0$ for $D<1/b$, it is bounded
for $1/b\le D<1$, and~\eqref{eq:s6-bound} makes $F_W(s)=\int_0^\infty A_1^\nu(D)D^{-s}\,dD/D$ holomorphic on $\Re s>11/12$. For
$\Re s>1$, Fubini's theorem as in Lemma~\ref{lem:b1-mellin}(i), complete multiplicativity of $\alpha\nu$ on
primary elements, and~\eqref{eq:s6-euler} give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:d196}
 F_W(s)=\widehat W(s)\sum_n\frac{\muk(n)\alpha(n)\nu(n)}{\N(n)^s}
 =\widehat W(s)\prod_{\pi\notin S}\Bigl(1-\frac{\overline{\psi((\pi))}}{\N(\pi)^s}\Bigr)
 =\frac{\widehat W(s)}{L^S(s,\overline\psi)}.
\end{equation}
Here $L^S(s,\overline\psi)=L(s,\overline\psi)\prod_{\mathfrak p\in S}\bigl(1-\overline\psi(\mathfrak p)
\N(\mathfrak p)^{-s}\bigr)$, and the finite product is holomorphic and nonzero on $\Re s>0$. The function
$L(s,\overline\psi)$ is entire, by Hecke's theorem~\cite{Hec20}, \cite[Ch.~VII, \S8]{Neukirch} (see also
Lemma~\ref{lem:cm-growth}). So $L^S(s,\overline\psi)F_W(s)=\widehat W(s)$ on $\Re s>1$, and hence on
$\Re s>11/12$. Suppose $L(s_0,\overline\psi)=0$ with $\Re s_0>11/12$. Choose a nonzero nonnegative
$\phi\in C_c^\infty((1,2))$ and put $W(y)=y^{-s_0}\phi(y)$. At $s_0$ the left side vanishes, while
$\widehat W(s_0)=\int_1^2\phi(y)\,dy/y>0$. This contradiction shows that $L(s,\overline\psi)\ne0$ for
$\Re s>11/12$. Finally $L(s,\psi)=\overline{L(\bar s,\overline\psi)}$, so the same holds for $L(s,\psi)$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Remarks.} (1) All constants depend on $\psi$, through $S$, $L_S$, $\mathfrak G_S$ and $\nu$. No
uniformity in $\psi$ or in its conductor is needed, because the conclusion is made for one $\psi$ at a time.
(2) For $\psi=\psi_d^*$ the twist is not needed: then $\eta=\chi_\bullet(4d)$, and Section~\ref{sec:sixth}
reaches $\overline{\psi_d^*}=\psi_d$ through the row $u_d$. A general $\eta$ is not of the form
$\chi_\bullet(u)$; for example, its order need not divide $6$. That is why the twist $\nu$ is carried in the
columns. (3) The weights here are compactly supported, so Lemma~\ref{lem:s6-reflection} needs only the test
functions $V(y)=\sqrt y\,W(y)$ of Appendix~\ref{app:reflection}. The general log-Schwartz tests and the marked
forms of item~1 of Section~\ref{sec:78status} are not used.

\subsection{The shared high-side lemmas, and the route of~\texorpdfstring{\cite{OAI78}}{[OAI78]}}
\label{subsec:s6-shared}

\begin{proposition}[The shared high-side lemmas for $\mathcal F_{\rm CM}$]\label{prop:s6-shared}
We use items~2 and~3 of Section~\ref{sec:78status}, proved in Appendices~\ref{app:s2-hecke}
and~\ref{app:s3-detector}: Lemma~\ref{lem:cm-growth} with the CM logarithmic control, and the detector with the
label set $\mathcal X_u$ of Section~\ref{sec:78rows}. Fix $\sigma_0\in[7/8,1)$ and
suppose $\beta_*>\sigma_0$. Let $\psi\in\mathcal F_{\rm CM}$ have finite part $\eta$. Use the fixed data
of~\cite[Section~6]{OAI78} for $\eta$ (the group $T$, $b_*$, $\xi$, $\tau$, $\Xi$, $W_0$, $W_1$). Take
$S$ to contain also the primes dividing the conductor of $\psi$ and the primes of norm at most $P_0$, and use
the weight~\eqref{eq:cm-weight}. Make three replacements in the following statements of~\cite{OAI78}:
Definition~10.1, eqs.~(10.1) and~(10.2), and
Lemmas~10.2, 10.3, 10.4,
10.5, 10.6 and 11.1.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] The target $\eta$ becomes $\psi$, and ``every primitive finite-order target'' becomes ``every
 $\psi\in\mathcal F_{\rm CM}$''.
\item[(ii)] The scalar denominators $L^S(s,\eta\overline{\chi_\bullet(u)})$ and $L_F^S(s,\eta)$ become
 $L^S(s,\psi\overline{\chi_\bullet(u)})$ and $L^S(s,\psi)$. The corrections $\mathcal H_{\eta,u}$ and $H_\eta$
 become $\mathcal H_{\psi,u}$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:cm-rows} and $H_\psi(s)=\mathcal H_{\psi,1}(s,1,1/6)$.
\item[(iii)] Every clause about a principal target, or about a principal denominator of a unit row, is
 deleted.
\end{enumerate}
Then each of these statements holds, with the same regions $\mathcal D_1(\epsilon_0)$ and $\mathcal D_2$, the
same real exponents and the same order of choices. In particular $P_0$ can be fixed before $\psi$ so that
$\sup_{\Re s>7/8}|H_\psi(s)-1|\le1/2$, and $c_S>0$ is unchanged.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The proofs in~\cite[Sections~10--11]{OAI78} use the target in exactly five ways. Every other ingredient involves no
target: the Mellin weights, the Gaussian, $M(z)$, $\widehat W_1$, the kernels of
Lemma~10.2, the regions, the contour geometry, the exponent
$E_{\sigma_0}(d;R,g)$ of eq.~(10.15) there, and the order of choices in Lemma~11.1.

\emph{(a) The correction.} The definition and the lemmas use the holomorphy of $\mathcal H_{\eta,u}$ in the two
regions, the bound $\mathcal H_{\eta,u}\ll q_u^\epsilon$ for all heights, and
$\mathcal H_{\eta,1}=1+O(P_0^{-4/5})$ uniformly in the unit phases (\cite[Sections~10.1 and~10.5]{OAI78}).
Proposition~\ref{prop:cm-rows} gives the same three properties for $\mathcal H_{\psi,u}$. Its local bounds use
$\psi(p)$ only through $|\psi(p)|=1$, so the same $P_0$, chosen before the target, gives
\cite[eq.~(10.2)]{OAI78}, and $H_\psi$ is holomorphic on $\Re s>7/8$. The constant
$c_S=\widehat W_1(1)M(1/6)(\operatorname{Res}_{v=1}\zeta_K^S(v))^2/6$ does not involve the target.

\emph{(b) The global reciprocal bound.} On lines $\Re s=\beta_*+v$, $v\in\{e,20e\}$, the denominators are
bounded by an arbitrarily small power of the conductor times a fixed polynomial in the height (\cite[proof of Lemma~10.3; proof of Lemma~10.5; proof of Lemma~10.6]{OAI78}). \cite{OAI78} takes this from
Lemma~4.9, whose global part uses only the definition of $\beta_*$, and from
Lemma~4.10. The primitive character inducing $\psi\overline{\chi_\bullet(u)}$ lies in
$\mathcal F_{\rm CM}$, by closure under finite-order twists (Section~\ref{sec:78}). So its zeros have real
part at most $\beta_*$. Its conductor divides the fixed conductor of $\psi$ times that of $\chi_\bullet(u)$,
whose norm is $\ll q_u$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:s3-labels}(e). The deleted Euler factors lie over $S$ and the primes
dividing $u$, and Lemma~4.10 only uses $|a_p|\le1$. Item~2 then gives the same bound.

\emph{(c) The bin ceiling $a\le\beta_*$} (\cite[proof of Lemma~10.3]{OAI78}, from \cite[Section~8.1]{OAI78}). By item~3, the bins are formed with the
label set $\mathcal X_u$. Its finite-order members have no zeros in $\Re s>7/8$ \cite[Theorem~1.1]{OAI78},
and $7/8\le\sigma_0<\beta_*$. Its other members are, after passing to primitive characters, members of
$\mathcal F_{\rm CM}$ or their conjugates. Their zeros have real part at most $\beta_*$, since
$L(s,\overline{\psi'})=\overline{L(\bar s,\psi')}$. Hence $M_i(u)\le\beta_*$ and $a\le\beta_*$.

\emph{(d) Principal clauses.} Three passages allow a principal character: a principal denominator for a unit
row (\cite[Section~10.1; proof of Lemma~10.6]{OAI78}), and a principal target with a pole of $L_F^S(s,\eta)$ at $s=1$ (\cite[proof of Lemma~10.5]{OAI78}). A member of $\mathcal F_{\rm CM}$ has infinity type $\bar z/|z|$, which is nontrivial. So neither
$\psi$ nor any $\psi\overline{\chi_\bullet(u)}$ is principal, and $L(s,\psi)$ is entire
(Lemma~\ref{lem:cm-growth}). These clauses are vacuous. The numerators $\chi_\bullet(u)$ do not involve the
target, so the classification of $u=1$ as the only row with a principal numerator (\cite[Section~10.1]{OAI78}) is unchanged.

\emph{(e) The numerators.} $L^S(w,\chi_\bullet(u))$ has finite order and does not involve the target.
Lemma~4.8 and the reflected bound of the detector apply to it unchanged (\cite[Section~10.5; proof of Lemma~10.6]{OAI78}).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Sextic row count with CM labels]\label{lem:s6-sextic}
By item~3 of Section~\ref{sec:78status} (Appendix~\ref{app:s3-detector}),~\cite[Prop.~9.2]{OAI78}
holds for the rows
of the CM probe, with the bins and witnesses of the detector with label set $\mathcal X_u$, the same envelope
$R(\delta)=\min\{1,\max(1-\delta/2,4/3-\delta)\}$ and the same order of choices.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The proof in~\cite[proof of Proposition~9.2]{OAI78} uses the witness in three ways: through its label, which lies in a fixed
finite set; through its length and lower bound, \cite[eq.~(9.17)]{OAI78}; and through the form of its
coefficients. For a CM label the inverse witness of Section~\ref{sec:78rows} is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:d197}
 M_u(D;W)=D^{-1/2}\sum_n\muk(n)\alpha(n)^{\epsilon}\theta(n)\chi_n(u)^{\epsilon}W(\N(n)/D),
 \qquad\epsilon=\pm1,\ \theta\in\Theta_0 .
\end{equation}
Freeze $v=\upsilon s_{\mathfrak s}w$ as in~\cite[proof of Proposition~9.2]{OAI78}. The exact identity
$\chi_n(u)^\epsilon=\chi_n(v)^\epsilon\chi_n(k)^\epsilon$ (\cite[eq.~(9.20)]{OAI78}) makes the row sum that of~\cite[Lemma~9.1]{OAI78}.
Its coefficients are $c_n=D^{-1/2}\muk(n)\alpha(n)^\epsilon\theta(n)\chi_n(v)^\epsilon W(\N(n)/D)$. These do not depend on the
residual row $k$, and $\sum_n|c_n|^2\ll1$ because $|\alpha(n)|=1$. The sieve allows arbitrary coefficients. The
remaining steps do not involve the coefficients: the count of powerful parts, the Sobolev step in the profile
parameters, and the optimization \cite[eq.~(9.24)]{OAI78}. The label set is still finite, of size at
most $4|\Theta_0|$.
\end{proof}

The sieve does not see the orientation of a label. Co-orientation (Section~\ref{sec:78rows}) matters only
for the inverse and plain moments of Section~\ref{sec:78moments}, which Part~I of~\cite{OAI78} does not use.

\paragraph{The route of~\cite{OAI78}.} Proposition~\ref{prop:s6-shared} and Lemma~\ref{lem:s6-sextic} also
give a second proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:s6-1112}, following~\cite{OAI78}. It needs one further input: the
unmarked completed-row moment at equal lengths,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:d198}
 \sum_{0<\N(m)\le C_mZ}\Bigl|\mathcal C^{(2)}_{V_{\rm G}}(Z;m,\nu)\Bigr|^2\ll Z^{1+\varepsilon}.
\end{equation}
Here $\mathcal C^{(2)}$ is~\cite[eq.~(5.30)]{OAI78} with
$\overline{\alpha(cn^3)}^{\,2}$ in place of $\overline{\alpha(cn^3)}$, $\nu$ runs over the fixed family
$\nu_\sigma\theta$, and $V_{\rm G}$ is the Gaussian test of~\cite{OAI78}. This is
Proposition~\ref{prop:cm-completed} with $Q=Z$. Its proof needs item~1 of Section~\ref{sec:78status} in its
unmarked form: a general modulus supported on $S$ and the non-compact test $V_{\rm G}$. The steps are then as
follows.
\begin{enumerate}
\item Suppose $\Delta_1=\beta_*-11/12>0$.
\item The low estimate of~\cite[Prop.~6.3]{OAI78} gives $|I_\psi(Z^{1/2},Z^{1/2},Z)|\ll
 Z^{1/4+\varepsilon}$: Cauchy--Schwarz in \cite[eq.~(6.2)]{OAI78}, the moment above, and
 \cite[Lemma~6.2]{OAI78}, which does not involve the target.
\item The Poisson representation is Proposition~\ref{prop:cm-rows}.
\item The detector is item~3, and the row count is Lemma~\ref{lem:s6-sextic}.
\item The central estimate \cite[eq.~(10.12)]{OAI78} holds with the same $E_{\mathrm I}(d)$. It uses the bin
 ceiling $\delta\le2\beta_*-1=5/6+2\Delta_1$ from part~(c) of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:s6-shared}, and gives
 the margin
 $m_c=1021/25000$.
\item The principal and outer rows are handled by Proposition~\ref{prop:s6-shared}, with margins $1/40$,
 $1/1200$, $43/300$ and more than $1$.
\item \cite[Prop.~11.2]{OAI78} then holds verbatim:
 $|J_{\mathrm I,\psi}-f_{\mathrm I,\psi}|\ll_\psi Z^{C_{\mathrm I}(\beta_*)-1/4800}$.
\item The continuation criterion, which holds for $\mathcal F_{\rm CM}$ (Section~\ref{sec:78}), with
 $\sigma_0=11/12$, $C=C_{\mathrm I}$, $\omega=\Delta_1/2$ and $\sigma=1/4800$, contradicts $\Delta_1>0$.
\end{enumerate}
The transfer to Dirichlet $L$-functions is not needed. This route needs items~1 (unmarked), 2 and~3. The proof
in Section~\ref{subsec:s6-twist} needs none of them; its only new input is Lemma~\ref{lem:s6-reflection}, which
is proved above from~\cite[App.~A.2]{OAI1112}. That lemma is the part of item~1 that concerns the modulus, for
compactly supported tests.

\paragraph{Summary.} Theorem~\ref{thm:s6-1112} is proved in Section~\ref{subsec:s6-twist}. The proof uses
Part~I of these notes and the steps of~\cite{OAI1112} for a general twist and a general excluded set
(Lemmas~4.4, 5.3, 6.3 and~6.6, Section~7, App.~A.2). It also uses Hecke's theorem~\cite{Hec20} and the prime number theorem~\cite{Lan03}.
Proposition~\ref{prop:s6-shared} and Lemma~\ref{lem:s6-sextic} use items~2 and~3, proved in
Appendices~\ref{app:s2-hecke} and~\ref{app:s3-detector}; the second route also uses the unmarked part of item~1
(Lemma~\ref{lem:s1-unmarked}).

\paragraph{Numerical checks.} We checked the new identities numerically.
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{The paired identities over $\mathfrak G_S$.} We took $\nu$ of order $12$ modulo
 $\pi=4+3\omega$ (norm $13$), and worked on the quotient $(\OO/4\OO)^\times\times(\OO/\pi)^\times$ of $\mathfrak G_S$,
 through which $G$, $\mathcal R$, $\chi_\bullet(-1)$ and $\nu$ factor. The forward
 identity~\eqref{eq:b2-forward} with $\xi_1=\overline\nu$ and the reverse identity~\eqref{eq:b2-reverse} hold as
 literal sums over all $144$ characters, with error at most $1.2\cdot10^{-14}$.
\item \emph{The twisted reduction.} The exact form $\mathcal M_D=Z+\sum_\xi c_\xi(\overline\nu)\mathcal S_\xi$ of
 the first Poisson reduction for the twisted rows holds to relative error $1.5\cdot10^{-14}$ at
 $(D,H)=(120,170)$, $(120,40)$ and $(200,300)$.
\item \emph{Exponents.} The exponent bookkeeping of~\cite{OAI78}, Part~I, holds in exact arithmetic:
 $E_{\mathrm I}(1/2)$, the comparison under the bin ceiling, $m_c$, the outer and principal margins, and the
 loss budget.
\item \emph{Rows and characters.} For each unit $u\ne1$ the character $\chi_\bullet(u)$ is nonprincipal, and
 $\psi=\overline\alpha\overline\nu$ has infinity type $\bar z/|z|$ and is not of finite order.
\end{itemize}
